(b) Keep `NAME=value` and refuse only when the value carries a newline. (c) Use the delimited form with a fixed delimiter string. (d) Stop publishing to `$GITHUB_ENV` and let every later step resolve the browser itself. | The `NAME=value` form terminates at the first newline, so a value carrying one appends a second assignment to the environment file and every later step of the job inherits it; because `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE` is caller-supplied, that is an environment-injection primitive rather than a formatting defect. (a) rests on escaping a form that has no escape syntax. (b) closes the assignment-form injection but still publishes a value the delimited form splits across lines. (c) is injectable by any value containing the fixed delimiter. (d) forfeits what the preflight exists for — one resolution shared by the whole job. Measured: with `GITHUB_ENV` pointed at a scratch file and a real Chrome path, the file receives the three-line delimited block; with `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE` set to a real path followed by a newline and `GITHUB_TOKEN=pwn`, the script exits 1 and no `GITHUB_ENV` file is created. | The delimiter is drawn from `crypto.randomBytes`, so a platform CSPRNG failure throws instead of publishing — a hard failure rather than a silent weakening. The CR/LF rejection is absolute: a path legitimately containing a line break, which neither filesystem in use here permits, would be refused rather than quoted. |
| D336 | **Closes the counting half of finding M2.** Re-derive every arithmetic claim the Rule 1 and Rule 2 documents make, in `backend/tests/test_docs_contract.py`, instead of maintaining the figures by hand: the `.gitignore` census and its anchored/unanchored split, the manifest pin census and the case arithmetic built on it, the §F row count in two places, the withdrawn ids, id uniqueness, path ownership in both directions, the completeness-table arithmetic, the layer labels and `REQUIRED` artifacts the extractor emits, the deck's `<section>` count, that every document quoting the measured commit quotes the same one, and that the dashboard's retention panel lists every `upload-artifact` the workflow performs. | (a) Correct the seven wrong figures and leave them hand-maintained. (b) Generate the whole of §F from a filesystem walk at build time. (c) Put the check in `docs/testing/dashboard-extract.py`, which already reads these documents' inputs. (d) Add a lint step to `ci.yml` rather than a test. | Every figure the finding named was correct when written and wrong when read, which is the signature of a hand-maintained count rather than of carelessness, so (a) fixes the instances and not the class — and this checkpoint's own code changes invalidated four more of them while it was in progress. (b) would make the census a build product and lose the one thing §F carries that a walk cannot: what each artifact *covers*. (c) would put a documentation assertion in the producer whose own output the documentation describes, and the extractor is already the subject of `test_dashboard_extract.py`. (d) runs outside the suite that gates the branch. The suite form also pays immediately: it found the frontend readiness artifact named by the extractor's `REQUIRED` contract and by `ci.yml` but by no panel of the dashboard, and it found itself unowned by §F. Measured: 19 cases, all passing; five of them failed on first run against the delivered documents. | The module reads the filesystem and never invokes `git`, because the conftest guards refuse a child process while a test runs. So the three change-set figures only `git diff` can produce are checked for internal consistency — entries + 1 = operations = distinct paths, and distinct paths − named paths = the four stated exceptions — rather than re-derived: a coordinated error in all three would pass. The provenance case likewise proves the documents agree on one hash, not that the hash names a commit that exists. |
| D337 | **Closes finding M7. Supersedes the per-slide figures D205 and D219 recorded.** Compress all eight content slides and the closing slide of `blitzy-deck/executive-summary.html` under Rule 4's caps, and make the caps a **test** rather than a claim: `backend/tests/test_docs_contract.py` re-measures every slide and fails when one drifts over. | (a) Compress by hand again and record the new counts in this log, which is what D205 and D219 did. (b) Split the over-long slides across more sections, up to Rule 4's eighteen-section ceiling. (c) Move the surplus into `<aside class="notes">` speaker notes. (d) Read the cap as excluding table cells, KPI labels and diagram labels, and change nothing. | The delivered file measured 47, 102, 39, 132, 40, 151, 121 and 65 body words while D205's own risk cell claimed 28, 32, 32, 37, 29, 37, 35 and 35: the log had drifted from the file and nothing re-derived either figure, so a stale count read exactly like a current one. (a) reproduces that failure mode. (b) is arithmetically closed - sixteen of the eighteen sections are already used, three slides needed splitting, and splitting to satisfy a word cap is padding. (c) hides the reading burden instead of removing it, because a note is text in the same file. (d) is contradicted by the review, which counted them: four candidate tokenisations bracket its published figures and every one of them counts the raw diagram source, so the enforced measure takes the **maximum** of that family - heading counted, hyphenated words split, Mermaid source counted - and a slide inside the cap there is inside it on every looser reading. | Compression is real and the deck now carries less: the three-column risk table became two columns, the reuse-and-addition sentence and the residual-risk sentence are gone, and the five documented limits are five two-word diagram nodes. Each of those facts is stated in full in this log and in `DASHBOARD-TEMPLATE.md`, and the deck names both documents. Counts on the strictest reading are 29, 36, 29, 36, 28, 31, 38, 33 against a cap of 40, so the smallest margin is two words and a future sentence added anywhere fails the test until this row is re-measured with it - which is the intent, and will still surprise. Two defects were corrected in passing and are not separate rows: the architecture flowchart's edges pointed at five node ids (`L1`-`L5`) that the diagram never defined, so Mermaid was inventing five empty boxes, and the enforcement table still described the frontend readiness check as "not yet a pipeline step" after D333 made it one. |

## §38 Comment and docstring remediation — what a comment in this tree is allowed to say

Scope: the fifteen decisions taken while closing the thirty-six findings of the final comment/docstring
review. The review's verdict was that rationale, history, rejected alternatives, per-test catalogues,
negative inventories and unretained run results had accumulated inside the code itself, in twenty-nine of
the eighty-five scoped files, and that several of those comments had gone on to contradict the code they sit
beside. Rule 1 names this document as the only home for "why", so the remediation moves the reasoning here
and leaves each call site stating what its code guarantees. Three findings were security findings and one was
a real defect rather than a wording defect; both are recorded below rather than folded into the comment work.

| # | Decision | Alternatives considered | Why this choice | Risks it carries |
|---|----------|-------------------------|-----------------|------------------|
| D338 | Adopt one explicit comment doctrine for the whole tree and apply it to all twenty-nine failing files: a comment may state a **contract** (what a caller may rely on), an **invariant** (including an ordering that is load-bearing), a **non-obvious production fact an oracle rests on**, or an **API signature obligation** (`@param`, `@returns`, `@throws`). It may not state why an alternative was rejected, what an earlier revision did, what a test below asserts, what a file does *not* do, or what a run once measured. | (a) Reduce every flagged file to a target comment percentage. (b) Delete every comment the review named and stop there. (c) Rewrite only the comments that are provably false and treat density as style. | A percentage is not the property the review was judging: the fifty-six files it passed reach comparable and higher density — `backend/tests/integration/conftest.py` is 72.8% comment lines, `e2e/tests/harness-fixtures.ts` 57.9%, `backend/tests/factories.py` 57.7% — so a ratio target would have deleted load-bearing invariants in the passing files' own idiom while leaving the flagged content classes intact. (b) throws away contracts that happen to sit in a flagged range, which the review's own resolutions explicitly ask be kept. (c) leaves the Rule 1 violation, which is the finding with twenty-plus instances. Naming the admissible categories makes the next review's question answerable per comment rather than per file. | A category judgement is still a judgement, so two readers could disagree about a given sentence. Bounded by the categories being stated here and by the flagged content classes being enumerated as exclusions rather than described. |
| D339 | **Superseded by D292, which raised the pin to 3.5.0 before this row was written, so the exception has no subject in the delivered manifest. The doctrine below still binds: a manifest comment may not present a vulnerable pin as safe.** State the `python-jose[cryptography]` pin in `backend/requirements-dev.txt` as a four-line **frozen-pin security exception** that says the version is known-vulnerable, that it is not treated as safe, and where the advisories and the owner are recorded — and nowhere claim the vulnerabilities are unreachable. | (a) Keep the reachability analysis, which is accurate. (b) Delete the comment entirely and leave the bare pin. (c) Raise the pin to 3.4.0 and delete the exception. | "Not reachable from this suite" is a true statement about the *test* suite and worthless as a *production* control, and a reader scanning a manifest reads it as an assurance that the dependency is safe here. That is the reading the security finding objected to, and the fix is to remove the assurance rather than to qualify it. (b) leaves a vulnerable pin with nothing marking it, so a dependency audit has no thread to pull. (c) edits a frozen specification this programme does not own — the same reason D227, D247 and D270 each declined it. | What shipped is D292's line instead: the pin is 3.5.0, above CVE-2024-33663 and CVE-2024-33664, and the one-line `# SECURITY:` marker states that positively. So the four-line block this row specifies is withdrawn for want of a vulnerability to disclose, not because the reasoning was wrong -- and the reachability claim this row objected to is gone either way. `backend/tests/test_dependency_closure.py` gates the pin at 3.5.0. |
| D340 | Reduce the `backend/pytest.ini` `addopts` comment to the contract — no coverage option here, scope and threshold supplied by the runner command, precision declared in `backend/.coveragerc`, both asserted by `backend/tests/test_coverage_gate.py` — and cite D195, D226, D252 and D291 for the reasoning. | (a) Keep the rounding counterexample, which is what makes the precision matter. (b) Delete the comment and let the gate test be the only record. | The counterexample is the *argument* for D291's decision and D291 already carries it; repeating it in the file is the duplication Rule 1 forbids, and the file only needs to tell a reader where the two halves of the gate live. (b) removes the pointer that makes the split discoverable at all — `addopts` naming no coverage option is exactly the kind of absence a reader cannot infer. | A reader of `pytest.ini` no longer sees why precision is set to 2. Bounded by `backend/.coveragerc` carrying that explanation at the point of declaration, and by the gate test failing if either half moves. |
| D341 | Correct `backend/tests/conftest.py`'s settings comments to the behaviour the prologue actually has — the twelve defaulted fields are **pinned** through `DEFAULTED_SETTINGS_ENV`, and only `NOTION_API_KEY` and the two undeclared consumer fields are removed — and delete the module docstring's contents catalogue, the sensitive-field risk argument, the disclosure history, the endpoint-authorization argument and the two records of what an earlier guard revision permitted. | (a) Correct the false comments and leave the rest, since the rest is accurate. (b) Delete the false comments without replacing them. (c) Change the code to match the comments. | The false pair was the worst finding in the file: `DEFAULTED_SETTINGS_FIELDS` was documented as removed from the environment while the prologue five hundred lines below assigns every one of them and says "Pinning, not removal" in its own comment, so the file contradicted itself and a maintainer reading the constant would have drawn the opposite conclusion about the oracle. (a) leaves a 52%-comment file whose history and arguments the review named specifically. (b) leaves the tuple undocumented although its role — feeding `MANAGED_ENVIRONMENT_NAMES`, and being pinned rather than popped — is not visible from its name. (c) is the tail wagging the dog: pinning is correct, because pydantic v1 reads `os.environ` before the `.env` file and removal would leave a `.env` on disk free to decide the value. | The file no longer records that the child-process guard was once narrowed to "while a test is executing", so the reason the allow-list exists is only here. Bounded by the comment retaining the invariant that the guard is unconditional in time, which is the property that history was there to protect. |
| D342 | Delete the exhaustive per-test catalogues from the module docstrings of `backend/tests/unit/test_db_firestore.py` and `backend/tests/integration/test_http_tweets.py`, and replace the firestore suite's "Every statement of the module is executed" with a pointer to the generated coverage reports. | (a) Keep the catalogues as a table of contents for a long file. (b) Keep the coverage sentence and add the measured figure. (c) Generate the catalogues from the test names so they cannot drift. | A catalogue of what the tests below assert is the tests restated: it drifts the moment one is added, it was already the largest single block in both files, and neither file's contracts live in it — they live in the isolation and divergence sections, which are kept in full. The coverage sentence is worse than redundant: it is a runtime result stated as a property, with no retained artifact, and the same measurement is regenerated into `DASHBOARD-TEMPLATE.md` from the run itself. (b) puts a figure in a source comment that a later test will falsify. (c) is machinery for a document nobody needs. | A reader wanting the inventory of asserted behaviours must read the tests. Bounded by both files keeping the divergence sections that state *why* each disposition is the oracle, which is the part the test names cannot carry. |
| D343 | In `frontend/src/test-utils/handlers.ts`, replace "Every value in the table was measured against the assembled application" with a reference to the two server-side suites that assert the same values, and describe `ALLOWED_REQUEST_ORIGINS` as the **explicitly handled loopback origins** rather than as "the origins jsdom serves the suite from". | (a) Keep the measurement claim and cite the commit it was measured at. (b) Retain the jsdom wording, since both origins are loopback and the distinction is pedantic. | A jsdom document has exactly one origin, so the old wording asserts something false about the environment and invites a reader to conclude that either entry would be served — when in fact the second entry exists so a suite that addresses `127.0.0.1` explicitly is handled rather than silently unhandled. The measurement claim has the same defect as D342's coverage sentence, and the fix is available for free because `test_route_surface.py` and `test_http_tweets.py` already assert every value in the table from the server side. | The table's provenance is now indirect: a reader must open the backend suites to confirm a cell. Bounded by both suites being named at the table, and by them failing if a value changes. |
| D344 | Reduce `frontend/src/test-utils/setup-jest.ts` to three stated invariants — the base-URL variable is deleted ahead of the imports, msw interception starts synchronously during module evaluation, and the global `afterEach` asserts the isolation ledger before it resets — and delete the "four things and nothing else" framing, the `onUnhandledRequest` callback rationale, and the "Not registered here" inventory. | (a) Keep the inventory, which stops a reader looking for a shim that is not there. (b) Keep the file as it stood; at 80% comment lines it is short in absolute terms. | The three invariants are the whole reason the file cannot be reordered, and they are the only part a caller can be broken by. The inventory is a list of absences, which grows without bound and is false the moment a shim is added; the callback rationale is a decision, and D108 and D238 hold it. (b) is the finding. | A suite hunting for a global shim no longer finds a comment confirming there is none. Bounded by `frontend/src/test-utils/setup-jest.test.ts`, which asserts the registered contract positively, and by `frontend/TESTING.md` documenting what a suite must install for itself. |
| D345 | Restate the jsdom-versus-browser split in `frontend/src/components/Analytics.test.tsx` from the code as it now stands: the real-browser chart path is reachable, and it is asserted by `e2e/tests/analytics.spec.ts`, which opts into the harness stub's forwarding header. Remove the claims that the harness stub rejects every route, that the browser path is out of reach, and that it is asserted nowhere. | (a) Delete the fourth table row rather than rewriting it. (b) Leave the text and add a note that the E2E layer has since changed. | Those three sentences were true when the stub rejected unconditionally and became false when D231 added the opt-in; a comment that sends a maintainer looking for an unasserted ceiling, when a spec asserts it, costs more than no comment at all. The row itself is worth keeping because the jsdom/browser boundary is exactly what stops this suite from simulating the path — (a) would lose that. (b) documents the drift instead of removing it. | Two files now describe one boundary, so a later change to the E2E chart test must update this docstring too. Bounded by the docstring naming the spec by path rather than restating what it asserts. |
| D346 | Document `getTrendData` in `e2e/harness/stubs/analyticsService.ts` by its actual conditional disposition — resolves when the response carries the forwarding header, rejects with one of three distinguishable types otherwise — in the module summary, the function summary and the `@returns`/`@throws` tags alike; and move the response-header channel argument out of the file. | (a) Keep "then rejects" as the summary, since rejection is the default. (b) Document the conditional only on `@returns`, leaving the summaries as they were. | The summary is what a reader of the public surface sees first, and "requests the trend series, then rejects" is a promise the function breaks under the one condition a spec cares about. A stub whose doc comment contradicts its own return statement is the failure mode the accuracy finding names. (b) leaves two of the three places wrong. The channel argument is a decision and D231 records it. | The summary is longer than a one-line summary wants to be. Bounded by the three rejection types keeping their own `@throws` tags, so the detail is where a caller handling failures will look. |
| D347 | In `e2e/playwright.config.ts`, name `e2e/tests/harness-fixtures.ts` as the owner of `test`, `expect` and `noEgress` at both places that had claimed a "Spec fixtures" section of this file, delete the reference to that section, and delete the worker-concurrency benchmark. | (a) Keep the benchmark and cite the host it was observed on. (b) Recreate a "Spec fixtures" section here so the comments become true. | D286 kept the fixture layer in `harness-fixtures.ts` and left two comments pointing at a section this file no longer has, which sends a maintainer to the wrong module — the same class of defect as D345, from a supersession rather than from a code change. The benchmark — a 64-core host, thirty-two simultaneous launches, a twelve-second serial pass — has no retained report, trace or run artifact, so it cannot be checked and must not be stated as fact; the cap it justifies is D214's and stays. (b) undoes D286 to make a comment accurate. | The reason the local worker cap is a quarter of the core count is no longer at the setting. Bounded by D214 carrying it and by the cap's own comment stating the constraint — one whole browser process per worker — that the arithmetic follows from. |
| D348 | Harden the credential evidence in `e2e/tests/configuration.spec.ts` rather than describing it as safe: key the fingerprint with a **per-run random key**, refuse to run unless every credential fixture matches the synthetic-literal contract, redact the one attachment that still serialised raw request bodies, and delete the comment endorsing full credential values in assertion failure diffs. | (a) Fix only the comment, since every value in the file is a test literal today. (b) Drop the fingerprint and attach presence and length only. (c) Attach nothing at all. | The comment was accurate about today and wrong about the property it claimed: an unkeyed FNV-1a digest of a short secret is recoverable by enumeration, so "length plus a stable digest" is not a redaction, and a run whose reports CI retains for thirty days is exactly where that matters. One attachment was in fact writing whole credential-shaped bodies, so this was a defect and not only a wording defect. The synthetic-only guard is what makes the remaining assertion diffs safe by construction, which is the half of the finding a comment could never satisfy. (b) loses the ability to tell which field differs between two runs, which is the reason the fingerprint exists. (c) leaves a failure undiagnosable. | A fingerprint is now incomparable across runs, so a flake that alternates between two values reads as two unrelated digests. Bounded by the guard failing the run before any credential-shaped value that is not a declared synthetic literal can reach an attachment. |
| D349 | Delete the `HUMAN ASSISTANCE NEEDED` block from `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and reduce the workflow's remaining comments to operational and security constraints with decision-log pointers. | (a) Keep the block and correct `requirements.txt` to `backend/requirements-dev.txt`. (b) Keep the block as a record that the workflow was reviewed. | The block asks a reviewer to verify a file that does not exist, in a workflow that has since been rewritten to install `backend/requirements-dev.txt` — so it is both stale and unactionable. The AAP's preservation exception covers the original route comments in `app/api/routes/tweets.py` and nothing else, so a placeholder in a file this programme authored has no standing. (a) preserves a request that the delivered workflow has already satisfied. | Nothing records that the workflow's install step was once unverified. Bounded by this row. |
| D350 | Reduce `.gitignore` to its section headings, its twenty-three active patterns and a two-line note that lockfiles are deliberately not ignored; move the absence history, the rejected ignore categories and the manifest-pinning arithmetic here. | (a) Keep the lockfile essay, because the absence of a rule is the thing a reader cannot see. (b) Delete the lockfile note too and rely on the contract test. | A `.gitignore` is read by people looking for a pattern, and sixty-eight per cent of its lines were argument. The note stays because a deliberate absence genuinely is invisible; the arithmetic behind it — three exact pins in `e2e`, eleven added to `frontend`, seventeen pre-existing caret ranges — is a measurement that will drift, and `frontend/src/test-utils/dependency-closure.test.ts` enforces the split it describes. (b) leaves a future contributor free to add the rule the test then fails on, with no comment explaining why. | The file no longer explains why `dist/` and editor noise are absent. Bounded by D287, which fixes the pattern set, and by this row naming the categories that were considered and declined. |
| D351 | Make every targeted-run example in `frontend/TESTING.md` and `e2e/README.md` fail-closed — `npm test -- …`, `npm run test:ci -- …`, `npm --prefix e2e test -- …` — and replace the `// … drive the subject and assert` placeholder with a complete assertion. The `--` is load-bearing in the prefixed spelling: without it npm consumes the flag itself and the whole suite runs, which `e2e/README.md` section 3 states beside the form. | (a) Keep `npx` and add a warning that dependencies must be installed. (b) Name the local binary path directly in each example. | `npx` resolves from the registry when the local executable is missing, so an onboarding document that shows it teaches a contributor to fetch an unpinned tool while believing they ran the project's own — and both guides already tell them the opposite in their install sections. A package script cannot do that: npm runs the binary from the package's own `node_modules/.bin`. (b) is correct but unreadable, and it hard-codes a path that a Windows contributor spells differently. An example ending in a pseudo-comment is not an example. | The examples are marginally more verbose, and `--` before a Jest or Playwright argument is a step a reader can forget. Bounded by every example in both documents now using the same form, so the pattern is learnable from any one of them. |
| D352 | **Applied except for the two content-security-policy residuals and the advisory-affected Mermaid pin, which stay at the point of use as well as here: D325 requires a reader of the policy to be told that `frame-ancestors` cannot be delivered from a `<meta>` element and that `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` is unavoidable under Rule 4, and a pointer alone does not carry that to someone editing the directive list.** Reduce the HTML comments in `blitzy-deck/executive-summary.html` to mechanics and structure — slide numbering, CSS section boundaries, rendering order and the concurrency note — and move the content-security-policy, font-integrity, prior-failure and residual-risk arguments here. | (a) Keep the security arguments, since a reader editing the head needs them. (b) Delete every comment, including the slide numbers. | The deck is a Rule 4 deliverable and its comments were arguing Rule 1 decisions inside the artifact, which is the violation regardless of how few lines it is. The structural comments are kept because they are how a slide is located in a 1,500-line single file — the review named them as keepers. (b) would make the file unnavigable. | The head's security posture is no longer explained where it is configured. Bounded by the mechanics notes remaining, so what each directive does is still stated, and by the arguments for why being one document away. |


## §39 The suite-size figures the documents publish, re-measured on the delivered tree

Scope: two decisions about numbers rather than about code. Nothing in this section changes behaviour;
both rows exist because a figure a document publishes is a claim, and a stale claim is a defect whether
or not any test notices it.

| Row | Decision | Alternatives | Why | Risks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D353 | **Re-measure and restate every published suite-size figure against the delivered tree, and take each one from a retained artifact rather than from a run log.** The figures are: backend `1015` collected / `1012` passed / `3` skipped at `93.33%` (182/195); frontend `24` suites, `371` cases, `347` passed / `24` skipped; end to end `31` passed in `5` spec files; `1,390` passing across the three streams, which is the deck’s headline KPI. | (a) Leave each document at the figure it was written with, and note somewhere that the totals are approximate. (b) Restate only the totals a test asserts, leaving the prose figures alone. (c) Delete the figures and publish only the commands that produce them. (d) Publish a range rather than a number. | Each figure was correct for the tree its author measured, and every one of those trees was a subset of this one, so every published figure was **low** — the four backend readings alone spanned 774, 805, 847 and 911 collected against an actual 1015. A reader who runs the documented command and gets a larger number than the document promises cannot tell a healthy suite from a broken document, which is exactly the failure D307 was written to prevent, one checkpoint later. (a) keeps that ambiguity and adds an excuse for it. (b) leaves the onboarding documents — the ones a new developer reads first — as the least accurate artifacts in the tree. (c) removes information that is genuinely useful as an expectation to check a local run against. (d) is a range nobody can falsify. Every figure written here was read from `backend/reports/junit.xml`, `backend/coverage.json`, `backend/reports/coverage-gate.txt`, `frontend/reports/jest-junit.xml`, `frontend/coverage/coverage-summary.json` and `e2e/reports/e2e-junit.xml`, and the deck’s KPI was then confirmed in a real browser rather than in the source. | The figures move again the moment a test is added, and nothing re-derives the prose ones automatically — `backend/tests/test_docs_contract.py` gates the structural counts and the deck’s word and diagram census, not these totals. `docs/testing/dashboard-extract.py` is the standing answer: it reads all of them out of the artifacts on demand, so a reader who doubts a number here has a one-command way to settle it. |
| D354 | **Leave every figure that a decision row or a dated evidence row states as history exactly as it stands**, including the superseded totals inside D307, D309 and D333 and the perturbation readings in `DASHBOARD-TEMPLATE.md` §7.5. | (a) Update them too, so no stale number survives anywhere. (b) Annotate each with its current equivalent. (c) Delete the historical figures. | A decision row records what was decided when it was decided; rewriting its numbers to match a later tree would make the log agree with itself at the cost of no longer being a record — and D307’s own subject is the *practice* of re-measuring, which is unintelligible if the figures it corrected are silently replaced by newer ones. The §7.5 perturbation sweep is likewise evidence of a moment: those runs happened on the tree they name, and no later run reproduces them. (a) destroys the audit trail this document exists to be. (b) doubles the length of thirty rows to no benefit, since §1.1 and D353 already publish the current figures in one place. (c) is (a) with less ceremony. | A reader scanning for a number can find an old one in a decision row and mistake it for current. Bounded by every such row being explicitly historical in its own text, by §1.1 being extractor-produced, and by this row. |
